#4974. The Best Foot Baths for Soothing Tired Feet, According to Podiatrists

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

As a longtime distance runner, my feet can get pretty roughed up after a grueling workout—even with the proper running shoes. But since I don’t have the time or endless funds to regularly hoof it to my local spa for a foot massage or pedicure, a home foot bath is my go-to. The best foot baths allow you to soak your tired toes, with extra features like bubbles, jets, and massage functions to help your feet feel rejuvenated.

A good foot soak at the end of a long day or workout can do wonders for your mental health, too, ultimately supporting your overall health. But unsurprisingly, a lot of the potential perks from home foot baths revolve around your feet. These baths can help boost circulation in your feet and combat inflammation that could lead to pain, swelling, and overuse injuries if it’s left unchecked, says Bradley Schaeffer, DPM, a podiatrist and foot surgeon specializing in sports medicine in New York City. Plus, now that we’re at peak cozy season, shopping for a great foot bath for yourself or a loved one (it’s a great gift for her!) is a cost-effective way to give your feet some R&R.

Doing foot baths a few times a week can help to slough off dead skin that builds up quickly, leaving you with softer, less grody-looking feet, says Melissa Lockwood, DPM, a podiatrist at Heartland Foot and Ankle Associates in Bloomington, Illinois

Not all foot baths are created equal, and it’s important to choose one based on your needs. So, if bubbles make you feel uncomfortable (me, hi), a foot bath that simply swirls around warm water may be more your speed. We also tapped Schaeffer, Lockwood, and Suzanne C. Fuchs, DPM, a podiatrist at Luxe Podiatry in Jupiter, Florida, for their advice on what to look for when shopping for these foot health-supportive devices and to get their feedback on their favorites.

Using that guidance and our personal experience, we found factors like size, weight, and additional features matter when choosing a foot bath. At baseline, a foot bath is a container to hold water to soothe your feet. But there are plenty of specific features to keep in mind when shopping for a foot bath.

The Hospan Collapsible Foot Spa packs a lot of features into a device you can fold flat. A huge highlight is the eight groups of massaging balls that go to work to ease pressure on the bottoms of your feet. (These aren’t motorized, but you can run your feet over them for a soothing effect.) While it’s a good idea to start out with warm or hot water, the Hospan Collapsible Foot Spa has temperature adjustment, allowing you to ramp up the heat between 95 and 118 degrees Fahrenheit without having to toss in more liquid. An LED display keeps tabs on the water temperature, giving you real-time information on how hot it is.

As the name suggests, this foot bath is collapsible and drops down when it’s not in use, making it easy to slide into or under tight spaces. A few potential downsides to consider: Hospan’s bubbles aren’t the strongest you’ll find, and this is a pricier bath when it’s not on sale, although it’s usually marked down a few times a year. But if you’re looking to pamper your feet with a range of options and are okay with investing a little extra, this is a solid choice that will hold up well and does just about everything.

It’s hard to find a foot bath that checks all the boxes, but the Conair Soothing Pedicure Foot Spa Bath comes really close. This bath allows you to get a good soak with a few extras to take your experience to a new level. “This model features massage settings, which can enhance relaxation,” Fuchs says.

This bath has a modest price tag of $30, which is significantly less than some others you’ll come across. It also uses vibration for a massage and has a pinpoint massage tool in the center in case you want to work on a particular area of your foot. While there’s no bubble feature, not everyone is into bubbles (they tickle my feet, which isn’t my favorite).

Conair’s foot bath is easy to use and clean, and offers plenty of opportunities to pamper your feet. The Conair Soothing Pedicure Foot Spa Bath uses a toe-touch control to turn the vibration settings on and off, although there’s no remote. This bath isn’t powerful enough to heat up cool or room temperature water, but it does a solid job of keeping already-warm liquid at the same temperature.

Not every foot bath has a bubble feature, and some are stronger than others. If bubbles are a priority for you, Fuchs recommends the Homedics Smart Space Elite Foot Bath. “Known for its bubbling action, this foot spa provides a soothing experience,” she says.

The bath delivers plenty of bubbles, along with heat, to create a spa-like feel while you soak. It also has three pedicure attachments that pop into the center: a massager, brush, and pumice stone. Those attachments neatly fit into on-board storage when they’re not being used. An ergonomic footbed with massaging bristles on the bottom of the bath also helps cushion your soles while you rest. The whole thing folds up pretty small, and has a special locking system for when it’s filled up.

If you’re interested in a foot bath that will give you a shiatsu-type massage, this isn’t it. But if you love the idea of blissfully scrolling on your phone while your feet are surrounded by warm, bubbly goodness, the Homedics Smart Space Elite Foot Bath is the right choice.

Getting the temperature just right in your foot bath can be tricky. But the Sharper Image Spa Haven Foot Bath has temperature control, allowing you to punch in the level of heat you want up to 108 degrees and let it go to work for you. This foot bath is loaded with features, including separate bubble and heat options, and free-spinning massaging rollers on the bottom that allow you to control the pressure and speed. (The rollers are removable for cleaning.)

The bubbling action is delivered in your choice of four speeds, and there’s also a removable pumice stone to help get rid of calluses and dead skin. When you’re done with your foot bath, just open the drain plug to let the water seep out. The only major downside to this foot bath (besides the high price) is that it’s not collapsible. But that also means it’s sturdy enough to rest your feet on the side when you want to take a break—a feature you won’t find in baths that break down.

The Medical King Foot Spa has six sets of massaging rollers to help knead out stress and pain after a long day. Couple that with bubble jets, vibration settings, and a built-in heater, and you’ve got a recipe for a good time.

This relatively affordable foot bath comes with a remote control that doubles as a control panel, with clear buttons to adjust the massage mode and flip through three temperature settings as you kick back. You can also set a timer to cut off the features whenever you want. The foot bath is collapsible, so storage under your bed or couch shouldn’t be an issue. This foot bath delivers if you’re looking for a way to work out kinks in your soles without having to put in any major effort.

You can splash out on a bunch of different features when it comes to a foot bath, but sometimes you just want a standard soak. If that’s the case—and you want to save some cash, too—consider the Mantello Foot Bath. This barebones bath is as basic as they come, but it has a few features that elevate it above your standard plastic bucket.

The bath has a sturdy plastic design that won’t tip over easily. It’s also deep, giving you plenty of space to soak your calves, too. The base has built-in arch support and raised dots for a little something extra while your feet relax. The open design means it’s easy to clean, too. There are no bubbles, massagers, or built-in heaters. But for a mere $23, what do you expect? If you’re just looking to unwind with a solid soak, this is a great choice.

“Foot baths can offer several benefits for foot health,” Fuchs says. She lists off a bunch of potential perks, including helping your muscles (and mind) relax, improved circulation, removing the buildup of dirt and bacteria, soothing conditions like athlete’s foot or plantar fasciitis, and exfoliation. Of course, you don’t have to use a foot bath to have good foot health—but many people find that it can be a comfortable way to support it.

Should you use Epsom salts in your foot bath?

In case you’re not familiar with them, Epsom salts can help soften skin and support muscle relaxation. Fuchs recommends adding Epsom salts or essential oils to your tub to ramp up the relaxation and health perks.

The odds are good that you’ll be hauling a foot bath in and out of a storage spot on a regular basis. Most foot baths aren’t overly heavy, but some higher-tech options have more heft to them than others. (The baths we recommend range from 1 pound to 9 pounds.) Just take a peek at the weight to make sure you can comfortably carry your device around.

If your feet are on the larger side, you want to make sure there’s enough room for them to fit comfortably inside your bath. Also, that foot bath has to go somewhere, both when it’s put away and in use. Check the dimensions and compare them to where you plan to use your bath, along with the drawer or closet space where you plan to stash it, to make sure you have enough room.

This came up with every podiatrist we spoke to. “Feet can get gross and we want to be able to have a sanitized bath each time we use it,” Lockwood says. Schaeffer agrees. “Bacteria can grow in these, raising the risk of infections,” he points out. With that, it’s crucial to look for a foot bath that’s easy to clean. That usually means the bath can be disassembled for scrub-downs or at least have very detailed cleaning instructions.

This isn’t a requirement, but having the option to collapse your foot bath can be helpful if you’re short on storage space. Many collapsible foot baths can fold down relatively flat, and have locking mechanisms to keep them in place when they’re in use. But if you have the room, you might prefer the sturdier design of a non-collapsible foot bath.

Many foot baths have features to give you more than a good soak, like bubbles, jets, and massagers. Some will heat up the water for you, and others have helpful elements like remote control, rollers, and pumice stones. These extra features usually come with a higher price, so consider what you will and won’t use if you’re looking to save on a bath.

To choose the best foot baths, I first relied on my own personal experience with these tools, making sure to weed out options I’ve used that have leaked, broken, or just not lived up to the hype. From there, I reached out to three podiatrists located across the country for their feedback on what to look for while shopping for these baths. I also utilized in-house testing by Women’s Health editors for units we got feet into. Each foot bath was evaluated on size, weight, ease of cleaning, and extra features like bubbles and built-in massagers to create the final list. Keeping all of that in mind, these foot baths are worth snapping up to get your soak on.

She has a master’s degree from American University and lives by the beach.

#4970. Crypto hack losses equal 57 cents per $1 earned onchain: Ark’s Valente

Crypto hackers stole $8.7 billion so far in 2026, surpassing the projected annual onchain revenue generated across the industry, according to ARK’s research director.

Hackers stole 57 cents for every $1 earned onchain by blockchain protocols since 2017, according to Lorenzo Valente, director of digital asset research at ARK Investment Management.

“Security is the bottleneck,” wrote Valente in a Thursday X post, sharing a chart showing that $8.7 billion lost to crypto hacks year to date had already surpassed the $5.6 billion in estimated annual onchain revenue for 2026.

Since 2017, the crypto industry has lost about $26.9 billion to hacks and earned $47 billion in total onchain revenue, he added.

The recent $6.3 billion stolen in the Binance hack alone exceeded Valente’s estimate for full-year 2026 onchain revenue. Value lost to hacks is on track to exceed onchain revenue by year-end for the first time since 2020.

The crypto industry saw a string of high-value hacks this year, including September’s $387 million Bitget hack and the $320 million Liquid Network exploit, as well as April’s $295 million Drift Protocol exploit.

The mounting security exploits are weighing on the institutional appetite for DeFi. In an April research note, JPMorgan analysts identified bridge security as a long-standing challenge for the industry.

Total value locked in decentralized finance (DeFi) has fallen nearly 10% since the Binance exploit, from $85.9 billion on Oct. 29 to $77.4 billion on Thursday, according to DefiLlama.

#4968. The Best Travel Backpacks for Women in 2026, Tested by Editors

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

The right travel backpack helps you master the art of packing light, so you aren’t hauling unnecessary weight to Terminal B. It has enough space for your flight essentials—laptop, passport, water bottle, charging cables—and is comfortable enough to strap on for hours. Whether you need an upgrade to complement your new carry-on, are investing in a pack to carry you through your next adventure, or simply looking for a great Christmas gift, here’s what to consider before you shop.

Below, see the best travel backpacks for women to take on your next adventure.

Topo’s Rover Pack is truly multifunctional, and that’s why we’ve dubbed it our best overall travel backpack. Its thoughtful design, roomy compartments, and casual look make it great for someone who’s always on the move, whether they’re going to the gym, commuting to work, or packing for their next trip.

The backpack has a 20-liter capacity, spread across a large main compartment and a zippered pocket on the front. There’s also a padded internal laptop sleeve, two adjustable water bottle pockets, and more. Plus, it’s made with dual side compression and spacer mesh shoulder straps. While it packs a ton, the bag is midsize enough that it can be used as an everyday bag, too.

The backpack’s main compartment is designed with a drawstring closure that can be cinched closed, and then secured using the two exterior straps with buckle closures. This adds some reassurance that whatever you’ve packed inside won’t spill out, or be at risk of getting stolen on hectic travel days.

We love that this bag comes in more than 15 fun color combinations that will stand out from the rest of the plain backpacks. Bonus points that it’s made from durable recycled nylon.

Most of the best travel backpacks will cost you at least $100—and sometimes more. But the Matein Carry-On Travel Backpack is the exception. This bag is designed with the same features as the other backpacks in this guide for a fraction of the price—under $40. It has more than seven compartments, including a hidden pocket with two ventilation holes that can be used to store shoes up to a size 12 or dirty laundry.

Inside you’ll find a padded neoprene pocket that can fit laptops up to 17 inches. The pocket closes with two zippers that meet, allowing you to add a lock to that particular compartment if you want to deter thieves. One feature we particularly love (in addition to its multiple compartments) is that it’s designed with comfort in mind. The part of the backpack that lies on your lower back when worn is made with extra padding to avoid discomfort, and its straps are padded, too.

A chest strap with a buckle closure would have made this backpack perfect to help distribute some of its weight (40 liters can feel quite heavy to some!), but it does come with a luggage strap you can use to rest it on your carry-on or checked luggage while you’re on your way to your destination.

We also love the hidden pocket in the back to store your most valuable items, like money, your passport, or other documents. To pack the main compartment, you can open the bag up like a clamshell, which makes it easy to store your clothing and other travel essentials in a way that makes the most out of the space you have.

The North Face Borealis is a classic backpack that’s been around for decades—25 years to be exact, and many people who own the cult-favorite bag will tell you they’ve had one for years. The Borealis was originally designed to be used on the trails, but it’s so spacious and easy to organize that it has gained a reputation as a commuter and travel backpack as well.

Assistant social media editor Isabel McMahon says she’s been personally using this bag for years because it’s so comfortable and durable. “The padded back is essential for a long travel day,” she says. McMahon also says the bag’s internal laptop compartment (which fits a computer up to 16 inches) is “next-level,” as it sits a few inches above the bottom of the bag to protect your electronics from accidental drops. The inside also features other small pockets for a phone, documents, and other tiny valuables.

We particularly love that this backpack is designed from a recycled nylon and polyester fabric blend that’s both water-repellent and hard to rip. That’s one of the reasons the bag is so durable—it can truly last decades when cared for. The iconic bungee cord system in the front adds a stylish note that makes this backpack stand apart from other options, but the cords are also functional, too. These can be tightened to hold other essentials, like a water bottle, travel blanket, or jacket.

McMahon’s only complaint about this bag is that there are a lot of straps and clips, which can sometimes get in the way and accidentally snag onto other items, she says. Still, she raves that it makes the perfect bag for a weekend trip at 27 liters.

Another cool feature of this bag is that it’s made with The North Face’s FlexVent system, certified by the American Chiropractic Association. This means the shoulder straps and back panel are designed to contour to the curvature of your spine, so that the bag is easier to hold and there’s less of a chance of you experiencing soreness or fatigue.

Read More: Best Travel Essentials for Women

As denoted by its name, the Triple-Zip Backpack has three main zipper compartments: the main center compartment, the large front pocket, and an external padded laptop pocket that can fit up to a 16-inch laptop. Each pocket is strategically placed, so you have easy access to your laptop, hydration, and any small essentials without having to dig into the main compartment.

Lululemon’s Tech Canvas material features reflective details and is made from recycled polyester, which is water-repellent, making this backpack a great choice for travel. It also has a trolley-compatible sleeve to slip over your luggage while making your way through the airport.

We love the attention to detail built into the thick padded straps—they sit closer together on the back to evenly distribute the load and have an adjustable sternum strap. Plus, load-lifter straps secure the bag to your upper body and the cushy back panel provides lumbar support.

Hikers or outdoor adventurers will enjoy traveling with Cotopaxi’s Allpa 28L Travel Pack. It’s lightweight, made of rip-proof fabric, and easy to clean. Additionally, it’s waterproof, which also makes it a solid choice for those that will face a variety of weather conditions. The Allpa is the backpack I traveled with when I trekked through the Amazon in Ecuador for two days. While there, I regularly had to lay the backpack on the ground for rest and other reasons, so it did get quite dirty. However, I loved that all it took was a good wipe-down to make the bag look brand-new again. I love that it can be easily cleaned using a wet towel, which makes it a solid choice for trips where it can get soiled easily.

Even better? The backpack is made with 100 percent recycled fabric, but it doesn’t skimp on durability. This bag was regularly grazed by tree branches during my hikes or dragged across a floor in my quest to unpack it, and it didn’t experience any rips. It’s made with many of the same features as the other backpacks in this guide—different-sized compartments, a water bottle mesh pocket on the side, and a rollerbag sleeve for resting it on luggage—but it also features four webbing loops along each corner of the bag, which are perfect for attaching gear, like a jacket, clip-on speaker, or a flashlight.

My only gripe with the backpack is that it has so many compartments I lost track of where I put things sometimes. But now that I’ve used it several times, that’s no longer an issue. It’s something first-time users can definitely experience, though.

While the Osprey Fairview is a much pricier buy than the Matein, it’s a lot more supportive (and long-term durable), making it well worth the splurge. Meg Wubbenhorst, a travel expert who has traveled to five continents and over 40 countries, says she regularly used the 40-liter backpack during her 20 months of solo travel, even though she originally thought it looked too small.

“I was pleasantly surprised at the roominess—I sometimes felt like Mary Poppins when unpacking, with ample room for all of my clothes, shoes (no more than 3 pairs!), and various travel accessories,” she says.

From the compression straps on the outside and inside of the bag to the countless mesh compartments and laptop sleeve, this bag makes it easy to get organized, but it’s the backpack’s support features that are impressive. The Osprey bag is made with an adjustable sternum strap that comes with a whistle (particularly useful for adventurers), and a hip belt that helps evenly distribute the bag’s weight to remove strain on your back and shoulders.

It’s also built with Osprey’s proprietary suspension system, which is designed to keep the backpack close to your body, while spreading its load across the entire back panel. This prevents the bag’s weight from pulling you back, so that the bag moves with you as you travel and not against you.

Additionally, whenever you do get tired of carrying this workhorse on your back, you can use either of the convenient carrying handles on its top and left sides. Wubbenhorst also says that she particularly likes that this bag passes most airlines’ carry-on policy, even when it’s stuffed to the gills.

The Kroser Laptop Backpack is a great option for those looking for a backpack for daily use, or those who regularly go on overnight stays. It’s also a great pick for those who prefer to pack their travel backpack with just the essentials—documents, travel blanket, etc. It only holds 37 liters and features multiple pockets for easy organization, including compartments for a laptop, water bottle, pens, and valuables.

Alexandra Dubakova, head of marketing at FreeTour.com, a website for booking local tours, personally likes the Kroser backpack because it’s designed with an aqua blue lining that’s more unique than the usual basic black or brown, and, in her opinion, a lot more functional, too. “That pop of color makes it so much easier to spot my stuff inside the bag,” she says. “I’m always digging around to find things, so having that brighter interior is super handy.” It also features a collapsible steel frame box opening that can give you a bird’s-eye view of what’s in the bag.

On the downside, she doesn’t love that the zippers are bulky and visible, since they make the pockets an easy target for thieves. The bag allows you to secure the two handles with a button once it’s zippered up, though, adding an extra level of security that can provide some extra reassurance that your items will remain safe.

If you’re looking for a backpack that easily fits under your airplane seat, Away’s Active Backpack is a solid choice. It’s also a great option for those that will be taking a backpack on their daily excursions once they arrive at their destination.

First, style-wise the bag is very chic. It’s designed for the outdoors, but it’s stylish enough to take along on your metro adventures without fear it’ll clash with your outfit and be an eyesore in photos. It’s also made from abrasion-resistant material that holds up against the elements—so there’s no problem using this one in rain or snow. While on a trip to Boston, I used this bag during an unexpected flurry of snow, and the bag quickly dried without incident to any of my belongings.

I also found it to be super lightweight. I wore it on the subway to go catch my bus to Boston from New York, and I had no complaints. I packed it with quite a few things—my laptop, some full-sized beauty products, and two days of outfits—and I never felt that the bag was so heavy that I had to put it down to rest.

The Away bag features several compartments, but my favorite is the hidden pocket in the back behind the bag’s straps. I tend to get paranoid that my wallet will get swiped, but storing it back there reassured me that even the savviest thief would likely not find it. Its thoughtful design and lightweight feel make it the perfect everyday bag for longer trips, but it can be used on its own for overnight stays or weekend trips.

What stands out about this Calpak backpack is that it doubles as a duffel bag if you get tired of carrying it on your back. The backpack comes with a removable longer strap you can use to hold the duffel on a shoulder. It also comes with carrying handles—one on the top and one on the side—so you can hold it in whatever orientation feels most comfortable.

Like most of the other backpacks in this guide, it features a ton of pockets, including a zippered water bottle pocket on its left side, a laptop sleeve, an internal mesh pocket, two small zippered compartments in the front of the bag, among several others strategically placed inside the bag. The Terra is designed with a clamshell opening, which makes it relatively easy to pack, and two compression straps in the main compartment designed to hold your contents in place.

Because of its 26-liter capacity, on its own, it’s spacious enough for three to four days of travel, but keep in mind that when it’s packed to the brim, we did find that it was a little tough to get its trolley sleeve over a luggage handle. An extra feature we would have loved to see is a waist strap. It does come with a sternum strap, but a waist strap would make this backpack even more comfortable to carry. This bag does get bonus points for being made from 27 recycled plastic bottles. Sustainability is always a plus.

When shopping for a travel backpack, most brands describe how much it can hold in liters. For longer trips over three days, a larger capacity bag—40 liters or more—is the better choice.

For shorter, weekend-long excursions, you can go for backpacks with a 25- to 30-liter capacity. If you’re using your travel backpack in addition to roller luggage, or as a day bag during your travels, you can go even smaller with 18 to 22 liters. “[Too much] weight can be a bit uncomfortable when traveling around Europe and other places, so I try to stay below 40 liters,” says Tamia Ramsey, a travel expert who has been to over 40 countries and founder of the budget travel blog, Poor in a Private Plane.

Ramsey recommends backpacks with four or more compartments, since they make it easy to organize clothing, electronics, valuables, and other travel essentials. She also finds a pocket in the front particularly useful for storing things you may need on the fly, like a pen.

Other features that can make a travel backpack even more functional are a water bottle holder, a shoe or dirty laundry compartment, and a pocket that isn’t too visible for protecting your wallet, passport, and other valuables from thieves.

Finally, if you want to offset the weight of your bag, you can seek options with sternum and chest straps. These straps typically buckle along the front of your body to redistribute the backpack’s weight and take some of the strain off your back and shoulders.

Travel backpacks can open in a ton of different ways, but some of the most popular options include clamshell, U-shaped, and top-loading designs. A clamshell design opens up like a book, similar to a suitcase. These are easy to pack because they give you a bird’s-eye view of what’s in the bag. Others are top-loading, which means they open up at the top, allowing you to pack from the bottom of the bag up. Finally, backpacks with U-shaped openings are made with a zipper that goes around three sides of the backpack. These provide a wider opening than top-loading backpacks. That allows you to access things quickly without having to lay your bag down on the ground, like you would a backpack with a clamshell opening.

The type of opening you choose depends on your preference, but ones with a clamshell opening are typically the easiest to organize and pack because you get such a great view of what’s inside.

When we first wrote this story in 2025, I considered my own travel experiences, feedback from other Women’s Health editors who travel for work and personal reasons, and spoke with three travel experts who roam the globe on a regular basis. Their intel helped me evaluate the following travel backpacks for women on packability, carry comfort, design, durability, and accessibility.

#4964. The Guy’s Guide to Adult Acne

We’re all stressed AF. The food we’re eating probably isn’t helping. Everyone told us we wouldn’t have to worry about our hormones going crazy after our teen years. What the heck is going on?

THE YEAR WAS 1997. I’d just made the varsity baseball team as a freshman, and I was ready to shoot my shot with every senior girl who’d give me the time of day. The only problem: I had also hard-launched my pepperoni face. I’m not talking surface-level New York slice stuff—these breakouts were Detroit-style-pizza deep. Every clogged pore had clocked in at the same time, and I was an absolute wreck.

For six months, I’d burned through everything the CVS aisles had to offer: Oxy Pads, Stridex, Noxzema, whatever stuff was hawking a promise of clear skin. Nothing worked. My face just got redder, drier, and more irritated. My mom (thank god for her) dragged me to the dermatologist, and I walked out with a prescription for isotretinoin, a.k.a. Accutane. Five months later, my skin was completely clear. My mini CVS stash went into the trash, and I thought that’d be the end of it.

I was wrong. I spent years assuming that because I took Accutane as a teenager, I’d paid my dues, but as a 44-year-old dude, I still get the occasional pimple or two—seemingly right before a big work meeting or whenever I have a lot of stuff on my plate. And it still messes with my head. Does my hygiene suck? Are my hormones to blame? Am I sweating too much? Apparently, I’m not the only one.

According to Christopher Bunick, MD, PhD, an associate professor of dermatology at the Yale School of Medicine, 83 percent of teen boys will deal with acne before they reach their 20s, which is often due to a surge in testosterone at the time. And while acne typically clears up as testosterone levels stabilize in our 20s, it can make a surprise comeback in our 30s, 40s, 50s, and beyond—with around 20 percent of men in their 30s dealing with breakouts, but only 12 percent seeking treatment from a medical professional. It’s not just a skin issue: Research shows that adult acne is consistently linked to a higher risk of developing anxiety, depression, and reduced self-esteem.

Acne (not to be mistaken for folliculitis, which can result from shaving) is mainly caused by a combination of excess oil production and dead skin cells that clog pores. This can create an environment where strains of Cutibacterium acnes—the bacteria naturally found on your skin—become imbalanced and trigger inflammation. This could look like blackheads and whiteheads, raised pustules and papules, or the deeper, more painful nodules or cysts that are more likely to scar. And despite what your instincts tell you, excessively scrubbing your face won’t help—it could actually harm your skin barrier. “You cannot wash away hormones,” says Dr. Bunick. Acne is an inflammatory condition, not a hygiene problem.

Scientists still aren’t sure exactly what makes some guys more susceptible to acne than others, but genetics and hormones are two leading factors. Research suggests about 80 percent of your acne risk is inherited. Your genes determine how sensitive your skin is to the hormones (i.e., androgens) that regulate the sebaceous glands and how aggressively your immune system overreacts to fight the inflammation. Then there are the lifestyle factors that play a smaller role but can collectively add up, such as diet (Dr. Bunick is a believer in the gut-skin axis—the idea that an imbalanced gut microbiome can drive systemic inflammation that can predispose you to acne), stress (this spikes cortisol, which also drives oil production and contributes to inflammation), sleep deprivation (same), and testosterone replacement therapy.

Though there isn’t a cure for acne yet, there are plenty of treatment options. “Untreated acne doesn’t just affect your skin. It affects how you move through the world,” says Dr. Bunick. “Acne impacts confidence, whether it’s making friends, having a social or dating life, or performance in work, sports, or other extracurriculars.” Earlier intervention means less of a psychosocial toll, less scarring, and (you heard the man) more confidence.

So if your bathroom still looks like mine did in the ’90s, it’s time to investigate what might be causing the chaos in your largest organ and learn how to treat it. The stories below will help you get your skin clear and, hopefully, keep it that way.

Bob Gearing is a New York-based writer covering men’s grooming from the perspective of a very real (and very sweaty) dude. By day, he’s a well-SPF’d tech sales bro; by night, he’s a red-light mask guy who definitely didn’t think he’d care this much about skincare. When he’s not testing the latest face serum, you’ll find him sweating through a round of golf or holding out hope that the Lions will make the Super Bowl before he dies.

#4959. Thailand proposes $148K daily cap on stablecoin deposits and withdrawals

Thailand’s SEC would set separate daily caps for stablecoin deposits and withdrawals and require customers to use wallets they own.

Thailand’s securities regulator has proposed capping daily stablecoin deposits and withdrawals at 5 million baht ($148,000) each per customer at regulated crypto operators.

The limit would apply separately to deposits and withdrawals at each operator, Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) said in a Thursday announcement. The regulator said the rules aim to curb money laundering, cybercrime and attempts to bypass cross-border money transfer rules.

The draft would bar stablecoin transfers to and from third-party wallets. Operators would have to verify that customers own the accounts or wallets they use for deposits and withdrawals.

Operators would have to comply with the Travel Rule, which requires crypto service providers to collect and share information about transfers, and use blockchain analytics to identify wallets linked to suspicious activity.

The draft would require crypto exchanges to name their market makers and identify the assets for which they provide liquidity. Crypto brokers would be barred from using liquidity providers for stablecoin-baht pairs. Certain off-platform trades would be subject to a 3 million baht minimum.

The draft follows a September consultation in which the SEC proposed stablecoin transfer limits and tighter oversight of crypto operators.

#4957. 7 Best Early Black Friday Mattress Deals, Vetted by AD (2026)

Black Friday isn't until November 27, but that hasn't stopped mattress brands from getting a head start on the holiday savings. If your current mattress has seen better days or you're waking up with more aches than you went to bed with, there's no need to wait until a designated shopping day to get a great deal on an upgrade. Savings are available right now.

Brands like Saatva, Casper, Purple, and Birch are already offering early discounts on everything from hotel-worthy innerspring mattresses to cooling hybrids and organic options. Whether you're a side sleeper in need of a little extra cushioning for your hips and shoulders or someone who prefers a firmer, more supportive surface, there's something worth considering before the holiday rush kicks into high gear.

Below, you'll find seven AD-tested mattresses, complete with reviewer insights, standout features, and details about the current promotion. Get ready to find your next bed without losing sleep over the price tag.

Saatva is giving shoppers who have “luxury mattress” on their Black Friday wish list a head start on savings. The brand is taking over $400 off its popular Classic queen. Designed to deliver hotel-worthy comfort at home, the innerspring mattress features a dual-coil construction; a plush, three-inch Euro pillow top; and targeted lumbar support that helps promote spinal alignment. It also comes in three firmness options—plush soft, luxury firm, and firm—making it a versatile choice for different sleep positions. Reviewer Erin Kaplan, a combination side-and-stomach sleeper, praises the mattress's supportive feel, durability, and temperature regulation. “Overall, I get a better night’s sleep on a more consistent basis since I switched mattresses,” she says. Complimentary white-glove delivery, a 365-night sleep trial, and a lifetime warranty round out the perks of this high-end buy.

Selecting a firmer mattress doesn't mean you’re opting for less comfort. That’s especially true with the original PurpleFlex bed-in-a-box, currently $100 off ahead of Black Friday. Unlike traditional memory foam beds, the PurpleFlex features a two-inch GelFlex Grid designed to relieve pressure while maintaining a supportive, responsive feel. Beneath that layer, a durable foam base provides stability, making the eight-inch mattress especially appealing to back and stomach sleepers who prefer sleeping on top of their bed rather than sinking into it. AD tester Chapin Rockwell particularly likes the firm construction, reporting “less back pain and stiffness” after switching to the PurpleFlex.

We’ve all been there: You toss and turn, flip your pillow looking for cool relief, and feel disappointed when you find nothing of the sort. If you tend to overheat easily, Casper's early Black Friday sale could be your chance to finally get a less sweaty night's sleep. Designed to keep nighttime overheating at bay, the mattress uses Casper's Snow Technology, which combines a pleasantly crisp QuickCool cover, phase-change material designed to absorb and disperse heat, and aluminum graphite HeatDelete bands that help draw excess heat away from the body. The mattress’s zoned memory foam and supportive springs also provide cushioning and spinal support without the overly plush sensation of an all-foam mattress. After testing the Snow Hybrid, contributor Kate McGregor describes its feel as "squishy when I lay down," while noting that the foam doesn't make getting out of bed difficult. She also loves its motion isolation and cooling qualities, although there were some lingering off-gassing smells after unboxing. Really, the only hot thing about this bed is its early Black Friday Deal: the sale offers over $800 off the queen-sized hybrid mattress, making now a great time to buy.

To make your bedroom a little more environmentally conscious, consider starting with the Birch Natural Mattress, a medium-firm hybrid model that’s free of polyurethane foam, fiberglass, formaldehyde, and pesticides. Instead, it’s made from natural and organic materials, including breathable organic cotton, natural wool, and organic latex that’s certified by the Global Organic Latex Standard (GOLS), breathable organic cotton, and natural wool. Individually wrapped steel coils provide responsive support, while the latex offers gentle contouring (as opposed to the sinking feeling you might get from memory foam). Contributor Zoë Sessums was particularly impressed by the mattress's balance of softness and firmness, calling its feel "Goldilocks perfection." Birch Natural also features reinforced sides for additional stability that you’ll enjoy as you sit on the edge of your bed to put on your shoes. Free shipping, a 365-night sleep trial, and a limited lifetime warranty add even more appeal to the brand’s early holiday sale, which takes 25% off sitewide, bringing the queen-size Birch Natural Mattress down to $1,399 from $1,865.

The WinkBed is a luxury hybrid mattress that caters to your preferred sleeping style, and it’s currently 30% off as part of the brand’s Veterans Day sale. What makes the WinkBeds particularly versatile is its four firmness options: Softer, Luxury Firm, Firmer, and Plus (the last level, Plus, is designed for sleepers who need additional support). Beneath its plush yet supportive Euro pillow top, pressure-relieving gel foam and individually wrapped coils work together to provide cushioning, targeted support, and motion isolation. A moisture-wicking Tencel cover also helps promote a cooler sleeping environment. But don’t just take WinkBeds’ word for it. Contributor Mark Burka notes that the mattress "provides strong edge support and wicks away heat," two features that help distinguish it from other hybrids. Moreover, it also comes with free shipping, a 365-night sleep trial, and a lifetime warranty, offering plenty of time to determine whether it's the right fit for you.

Side sleepers searching for a more supportive night's rest should put shopping Nolah's early Black Friday sale at the top of their shopping list. Shopping editor Audrey Lee, who tested an earlier iteration of the Evolution, praises its high level of comfort and cooling abilities. “I found this design’s contouring quality top-tier for pressure relief, but I still had ample support thanks to the coils,” she says. The brand is currently taking 35% off its mattresses, bringing the queen-size Evolution from $2,277 down to $1,480. Designed to cushion sensitive pressure points without sacrificing support, the luxury hybrid combines responsive AirFoam Luxe, individually wrapped coils, and targeted lumbar support to help keep the spine aligned. Shoppers can choose among Plush, Luxury Firm, and Firm options, all of which can accommodate an optional GlacioTex cooling pillow-top upgrade for additional cushioning and temperature regulation. A 365-night sleep trial, free shipping, and a limited lifetime warranty make the early holiday deal even sweeter.

Handcrafted in Illinois, this mattress combines three inches of GOLS-certified organic Dunlop latex for a breathable, responsive sleeping surface with organic cotton and wool that’s certified by the Global Organic Textile Standard (GOTS). (That’s a third party verification that ensures the organic origin and processing of the materials used in the bed.) The Natural Escape’s individually pocketed coil system features seven zones of targeted lumbar support, making the mattress a strong contender for shoppers who prefer a firmer surface. "I feel super supported," notes contributor Kristi Kellogg of her experience testing the Natural Escape. She also has zero back pain while sleeping on it and appreciates the straightforward delivery and setup process: It arrives compressed in a box and includes free shipping and returns, a 365-night sleep trial, and a 20-year warranty. Need another reason to consider making the switch? #4954. 7 Best Work Totes, Tested by Style Editors

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Finding the right work tote is surprisingly tough. It needs to be durable, secure, organized, comfortable, and, hopefully, stylish too. If you’re like our editors, you might be stuffing everything from water bottles to emergency umbrellas in your everyday bag. A good work tote may even be able to double as a carry-on or weekender bag.

While I work remotely, I’m often toting my work setup with me to my favorite coffee shop, coworking space, or a friend’s house to get work done. So, I know a thing or two about what matters when you’re looking for a durable bag that can securely hold all your everyday essentials (and more) for years without signs of wear and tear.

Work totes are some of the most functional bags you can own, too. The best work totes often reduce strain on your back and shoulders, since their handles make them easy to carry. They can also allow you to access the items you need most quickly. For example, most bags are designed with smaller pockets so you can easily store and grab your phone or wallet in a jiffy.

Not all work totes are created equal, though. A lack of functional pockets can make you feel disorganized, and straps that dig into your shoulders can make your commute miserable. Our Women’s Health editors tested more than 20 work totes and spoke to fashion experts to the find the best options for every budget.

From work to travel, the Apryl Laptop Tote Bag is the best overall work tote for its smart compartment placement, durability, and comfy straps. Even better: At $50, it’s budget-friendly, too. When our tester first ordered this bag, they didn’t have high expectations for it because of the affordable price point, but it quickly became the best tote they tested.

Our tester is obsessed with the bag’s comfort, which makes it easy to hold and tote. Made of faux leather, the bag has structured but flexible straps that don’t dig into your shoulders or hands, no matter how long you hold the bag. Inside the bag, you’ll find a padded laptop pocket that closes with Velcro to keep your device secure. There are also five additional smaller compartments inside (all cotton-lined), including a water bottle holder that’s also padded.

One thing I particularly love about this bag is that it’s also designed with two exterior compartments, one on each side of the bag, that each close with magnetic buttons. "I found these useful for throwing keys, and other items I don’t necessarily need throughout the day, like an emergency Tylenol," our tester says.

When you put the bag down, it lies flat, which makes it really easy to reach inside and find specific items. However, the bag doesn’t have studs at the bottom, so it does touch whatever you place it on. While the red color our tester received can hide stains or dirt fairly well, another color option, like the beige choice, may not.

I tested Quince’s Leather Shopper Tote, and it has quickly become my go-to work bag. While most bags on this list have a laptop pocket, this work tote has the best one. Its laptop compartment is lined in soft suede, which protects your device against scratches. I also like to throw my sunglasses in there, since I’m pretty bad about carrying their case, but I’m always afraid the keys in my bag will rough them up. What I love about this zippered laptop holder, which sits in the center of the purse, is that it also serves as the bag’s divider, essentially dividing the bag into two separate compartments. It’s almost like you have two purses.

I like using one side to store my work essentials, like my planner or other important documents, and the other for extras, like my makeup, hand cream, and, on rarer occasions, even a pair of shoes. Each side also has smaller compartments to increase your organization options: One side has two smaller compartments, while the other has just one. I do wish at least one of these compartments was zippered to keep small items, like my wallet, more secure during crowded commutes, but I just throw it in the same pocket as my laptop, and that works for me.

I also like that this bag is structured and more on the rigid side because it gives the bag a just-purchased feel, while giving me a more put-together look. I find that the straps are a little too thin, though, which, as a chronic overpacker, makes me worry about how much of a load the bag can really handle. I’ve had experiences where I’ve hung bags with thin straps onto hooks (like those inside a bathroom stall) only to have a strap detach from the bag, and it’s not the best experience. So while I do use some work totes for short weekend trips, this is one I would likely leave behind.

Still, it’s the perfect bag for professional settings or errand-running on weekends. Its price point of $150 is also exceptional for a bag made from real leather. Other options, like the Cuyana bag in this guide, are much more pricey.

Read More: Quince Italian Leather Tote Review

Amazon has a boatload of bags that look pricier than they are, and, at around $35, this work tote is just that. It’s designed with a crocodile pattern that gives the bag a stylish touch and high-end feel, which is what originally drew me to it, but it’s also one of the best-rated tote bags on the site. Like our best overall bag, it’s made with a padded laptop pocket that closes with a small Velcro strap, but there’s also a zipper compartment in the middle that’s big enough to store a laptop.

Inside the bag, you’ll find two small mesh pockets on the narrower sides, perfect for storing small valuables, pens, or travel makeup essentials. “On days I wore a watch to the office, I would take it off and store it in one of those pockets until it was time to leave (I find it hard to type and wear a link-chain watch at the same time because of the friction between my hand and the desk),” one tester says. “I also used those pockets to store cough drops, lip gloss, and other trinkets I didn’t want to get lost in the abyss of my work tote.”

There’s a smaller zippered pocket inside of the purse, which I appreciate for storing items you want to keep secure, like cash, plus two other smaller compartments for a phone, wallet, or other mid-sized items. For perspective, you could probably fit a bottle of perfume in each of them.

I also appreciate this bag’s versatility. It’s designed like an accordion, meaning it widens when stuffed to the brim, but appears narrower when it’s not packing a heavy load. The purse also comes with an additional strap, so you can detach the tote’s carrying handles and use it as a crossbody bag if that’s your preference.

Our tester’s only concern with this bag is that the gold accents on the straps may fade over time, which may make the bag unsightly. However, they have used it for over a month, including on rainy days, and have had no issues yet.

There’s just something about real leather that feels so luxe. That’s one of the reasons this leather tote from Cuyana is worth the splurge. The material feels soft and has proven to be durable, despite how much our tester stuffed this bag or threw it on the ground after a long day.

The bag features only one big compartment, which may be a con for some. (There is a smaller pocket inside—about twice the size of an iPhone plus—but nothing else besides that.) If you prefer a little more organization, you can buy accessories that attach to the inside of the bag using button clasps. These include zippered pockets of different sizes and a laptop compartment. “Though I don’t love having to pay more for these accessories, I do like that these make the bag’s design totally customizable,” our tester says.

Our tester used the bag without organizational pockets and was impressed at how much it could hold. “I’ve thrown a laptop, a pair of shoes, extra clothes, and my toiletry bag in there easily,” they say. “While I wouldn’t call this bag stretchy, its material is less structured, offering a little more give than other bags on this list.”

They particularly liked that the straps are built into the bag, instead of sewn onto it. “This gives me extra reassurance that this purse won’t fall apart on me when I’m on the go,” they say. “The straps are also pretty wide and flexible, so they feel very comfy on my shoulders, no matter how heavy the bag is.” It is a little tougher to wear this tote on your shoulder when wearing a thick, puffy coat, but it just takes a little more effort to throw on than some of the other bags on this list.

The Longchamp Le Pliage Tote is a cult-classic bag that you’ve probably noticed someone else carrying on your commute before. The large nylon tote with leather trim comes from a luxury French brand, which explains the higher price tag.

While it doesn’t offer a lot of internal organization—just two pockets—the large internal compartment is great for those who need to stash bulkier items, like an extra pair of shoes.

Plus, the water-resistant nylon exterior is flexible and durable, so you don’t have to worry about the elements as much as with an all-leather bag.

With the Le Pliage Tote, you’re paying for the Longchamp name and classic design, but you could definitely do worse than a bag as durable and functional as this one.

From one of our favorite luggage brands, the Monos Metro Tote is perfect for someone who travels frequently for work or fun. The vegan leather design is simple, yet stylish, and it comes in six colors to coordinate with your luggage set.

While it’s priced at nearly $250, the bag boasts the most extensive organization I’ve seen in a work tote, including a laptop compartment, water bottle holder, and handy exterior pocket for quickly stashing items during airport security. And the zipper closure to keep your belongings secure, whether commuting or jet-setting.

You may know Baggu for its reusable grocery totes, but the brand also makes durable purses and work totes in its signature slouchy, patterned nylon style. This shoulder bag is for someone who packs light and just needs a versatile, casual bag to carry a laptop and a few extra items.

The Baggu Shoulder Bag is a workhorse with a thick, adjustable strap, zipper closure, and a spot to attach your keys. The tote is completely machine washable, which is an anomaly among work bags. However, the bag’s main compartment is narrow with space for a 14-inch laptop and a slim notebook or two.

At around $50, it’s a great everyday bag and comes in a fun variety of colors and patterns, including leopard and gingham, for someone who wants to show a little personality at work.

While work bags are functional, we also want them to be stylish and versatile, so you don’t have to switch bags for a different outfit. Most of our favorite work totes are simple, timeless silhouettes with a few exceptions, like the Baggu Shoulder Bag for a slouchy look.

The material of your bag will often come down to style preference, but some materials are more functional than others. For example, canvas bags are durable, lightweight, and great for everyday use. That said, they do take a little more effort to clean when soiled, since canvas doesn’t dry quickly. You may need to give it some time to air out after spot cleaning.

Another option: Leather totes—these are chic and long-lasting. Just keep in mind that they can be pricier than other totes because they’re investment pieces that should last for years. They’re also versatile and stylish enough to take to post-work activities. (Read: You won’t look weird showing up to happy hour with this as your plus-one.)

Vegan leather is also a great option because it’s a more affordable and cruelty-free option, and some of them look pretty realistic. Just keep in mind that vegan leathers can be made from a variety of materials, and some are more durable than others. For example, some vegan leathers made from low-quality plastic are prone to peeling.

Finally, nylon or polyester bags are perfect for a more casual, sporty vibe. They are easy to clean and can really take a roughing up with day-to-day use.

Look for a bag with both larger and smaller compartments, which will make organizing and protecting your important items so much easier, says fashion contributor Rose Lauture. For example, the zippered laptop compartment in Quince’s Italian leather tote is lined with suede to avoid scratching it. Meanwhile, smaller compartments will prevent you from being forced to dig through all the random stuff that accumulates in a work tote to find your phone, pen, or lip gloss.

Think about how you use your tote bag when searching for the right size and organizational capabilities. For example, if you like to pack lunch or bring your gym clothes along, then you’ll need a bag with a wide open compartment for bulkier items. If your main goal is to stay organized and pack light, then a smaller bag with interior pockets may help you on the go.

The closure of the bag will depend on your needs. Totes with a zip or snap closure are great for securing your items and protecting them against thieves, Lauture says. It also ensures that your belongings won’t be scattered across the floor if the bag falls over. But if you prefer accessibility instead of security, open totes may be a more convenient option, she adds.

Having a work tote with comfortable straps can make or break your commute, whether you catch a train into the city or walk to and from your car. Look for bags with thick, durable straps that are tall enough so the bag doesn’t rub against your armpit. Some work totes even come with a crossbody option or adjustable strap, if you prefer to tailor the bag to your height.

I’m always working on the go, so I have a lot of experience carrying around work totes, from faux leather to canvas options. I did extensive research on work totes from top brands to come up with an initial list for this guide, choosing a variety of sizes, materials, and price points. I got hands-on with the runner-up tote from Quince, and one Women’s Health tester with more than 12 years of experience using work totes spent a month testing the other bags.

From commuting to weekend trips, I evaluated the work totes on factors like design, material, compartments, comfort, and more.

#4951. The 16 Most Festive Cities in the US to Visit This Christmas Season

Even the most steadfast Scrooge will find themselves humming a holiday jingle in these places that pack on the seasonal cheer. The most festive cities in the US for Christmas are fully devoted to their merry-making, with multi-weekend celebrations, European-style holiday markets, millions of twinkling lights, and Christmas tree lighting ceremonies for the whole town to enjoy. Some tried and true classics appear on this list—the Big Apple, of course, is included—but others might come as a surprise. You’ll also find a city that celebrates the holidays in 18th-century style, a Danish town famous for its architecturally impressive gingerbread, and a ski destination that even sends Santa down the slopes. Below, tour the 15 most festive cities in the US for Christmas.

We couldn’t put together a Christmas list without giving a nod to NYC. The setting of many a holiday film, the Big Apple is full of Christmas spirit, including the Rockefeller Center tree lighting ceremony, Christmas shows like the Radio City Christmas Spectacular, the ice skating rinks in Central Park, and holiday windows at the iconic department stores.

Dog sledding, snowshoeing, skiing, and snowboarding are a major part of the holiday activities in this alpine city, which is home to the country’s largest lift-served ski resort, Park City Mountain. The resort’s Town Lift even carries Santa into Park City for an annual celebration with cocoa, carolers, and tunes. On Christmas Eve, Santa returns for a torchlight parade as over 100 ski and snowboard instructors carve their way down the mountain.

Can’t make it to Copenhagen before Christmas? Instead, head to Solvang—a deeply Danish town only about 35 miles from Santa Barbara that puts a Scandinavian spin on holiday cheer. The city’s seasonal celebration is called Julefest, which takes place from late November through early January and features holiday markets, a parade, carolers, and holiday concerts. Be sure to swing by The Solvang Bakery, famous for its elaborate gingerbread houses.

If you’re dreaming of a white Christmas, you’re pretty much guaranteed to see some snow in Vail, where alpine sports are in full swing by December. In addition to hitting the slopes at Vail Ski Resort, wandering its idyllic village—with cobblestone walkways, Bavarian-style architecture, and tons of twinkling lights—will make you feel like you’re in a real-life storybook. Take part in an ugly sweater fun run, do some holiday shopping at Kris Kringle Market, and take in a free skating show, featuring world-class athletes.

This tinsel-loving town in southern Indiana, about 60 miles from Louisville, Kentucky, celebrates Christmas year round. Holiday World, an amusement park with rides named after Santa’s reindeer; Santa’s Candy Castle, a confectionary shop that dates back to 1935; and the Santa Claus Christmas Store, stocked with 8,000 ornaments, are open beyond just December. But the last month of the year is when the holiday vibes kick into high gear. Over the first three weekends of December, the town hosts its annual Santa Claus Christmas Celebration, including a Christmas parade, an arts and crafts show, and elaborate holiday light displays. The city is also home to the Santa Claus Museum & Village, where local “elves” answer letters to Santa for kids from all over.

Despite its warm temperatures and palm trees instead of pines, Charleston curates a ridiculously cozy holiday scene. One beloved tradition is the Holiday Festival of Lights at James Island County Park, which includes a spectacle of more than 750 illuminated displays (even the palm trees are aglow), a holiday train ride, and visits with Santa. Elsewhere, historic homes are decked in vintage decor, and residential neighborhoods like I’On—just 15 minutes from downtown—go all in, with luminary-lined sidewalks, horse-drawn carriages, and neighbors gathering at the European-inspired wine bar Saint Urban’s for post-caroling sips.

The Windy City embraces the holidays with travel-worthy Christmas markets. The most famous is Christkindlmarket, a European-style outdoor extravaganza with handcrafted gifts, mulled wine, and giant German pretzels. On Michigan Avenue, an illuminated nighttime parade, The Wintrust Magnificent Mile Lights Festival, kicks off the season; an ice skating rink opens in Millennium Park; and hotels deck their halls in elaborately festive fashion. For example, The Pendry Chicago transforms its rooftop bar, Château Carbide, into a winter wonderland with cozy chalets, each featuring a personal fireplace.

Take a trip back in time at Colonial Williamsburg, which celebrates Christmas with 18th-century flair. While in town, make sure to visit the Busch Gardens Christmas Town, decorated with eight million lights.

Asheville’s iconic Biltmore Estate, built by George Vanderbilt, is America’s largest home. How fitting, then, that it features one of the grandest Christmas displays in the country. More than 70 trees fill the 175,000-square-foot house for the holiday season. In 2023, Hallmark even premiered a fantasy rom-com inspired by the spectacular, appropriately titled A Biltmore Christmas.

Experience an Ozark Mountain Christmas in Branson, the Live Music Show Capital of the World. (There are, of course, many special performances throughout the season.) Stroll down the two-and-a-half-mile Shepherd of the Hills Trail of Lights, or see the five million Christmas lights and 1,000 decorated trees at Silver Dollar City.

Starting December 20, this town hosts the 12 Days of Aspen, during which local businesses offer holiday activities to visitors and residents, from designing your own gift wrap at the St. Regis to a performance of Charles Dickens’s A Christmas Carol at the Wheeler Opera House.

From November to January, visit the Gilded Age mansions in Newport to see them filled with Christmas decorations. Plus, the whole town partakes in Christmas in Newport, a monthlong holiday celebration established in 1971, where residents and businesses alike decorate and host events.

The crown jewel of the Jersey Shore, this seaside town is filled with quaint bed and breakfasts and charming shops. The Victorian houses that line its streets are decorated with wreaths, lights, and garlands—take a ride on a holiday-themed trolley or join a popular walking tour to see them all.

Who says you need to have snow for Christmas? In Newport Beach, you can enjoy a 108-year-old tradition: the Newport Beach Christmas Boat Parade. More than a million people watch yachts, boats, and kayaks sail through the harbor each year. Outside of the annual spectacular, the parks services helps further set the holiday tone, adding Christmas trees in unexpected places, like at the Crystal Cove State Park, seen here.

#4950. Bit Digital stock falls 5% in pre-market after Q3 revenue miss

Bit Digital’s $27 million third-quarter revenue came in 16% below analyst consensus estimates, marking an over $5 million shortfall.

Shares in Nasdaq-listed Bit Digital fell 9.3% in premarket trading on Thursday after the Ethereum treasury and AI infrastructure company reported third-quarter revenue below analysts’ estimates.

Bit Digital reported $27.1 million in revenue, down 11% from $30.5 million a year earlier. Net income attributable to shareholders was $28.5 million, or 8 cents per diluted share, according to its Thursday SEC filing.

The company’s revenue came in nearly 16% below analyst consensus estimates of about $32.2 million, marking a $5.1 million shortfall, according to Yahoo Finance.

Bit Digital reported $1 million in Ether (ETH) staking revenue, down about 66% from $2.9 million a year ago. The company reported holdings equivalent to about 163,726 ETH, worth $439.4 million as of Sept. 30.

Its Bitcoin mining revenue fell to $2 million from $7.4 million a year earlier.

Bit Digital said that the decline in mining revenue was “consistent with the continued winddown of the business.” The company revealed it pivoted its corporate treasury strategy from Bitcoin to Ether in July 2025.

#4949. I Spent Years Hiding My Ulcerative Colitis. One Night at Sundance Changed How I Saw My Disease

In an exclusive excerpt from her new book, NBC correspondent Chloe Melas shares the reality of navigating an early relationship with IBD.

For 15 years, I've battled a form of Inflammatory Bowel Disease (IBD) known as Ulcerative Colitis. It's an autoimmune disease in which your body attacks your colon. When I was diagnosed at 25-years-old after finding blood in my stool, I felt like I had nowhere to turn because neither side of my family has gut issues. I thought if maybe I ignored my symptoms (the blood, the bloating, the constipation), it would eventually go away. It didn't. As I continued to chase my career and search for love, things got worse and I eventually couldn't hide it anymore. When I went to the Sundance Film Festival I was at the height of my disease and I felt like it was following me like a cloud trying to rain on every moment of joy. What follows is a story—from my new book that I co-authored with Judy Batalion, The Girl’s Guide to Guts—that I never planned to share publicly. But I am now in hopes of helping anyone out there feel less alone. — Chloe Melas

As I rose up through the TV ranks, my invitations became better and better. I wasn’t at the end of the red carpets anymore; I was now just a few spots from the end! One of the best parts of the job is being able to attend some of the most exclusive events, like the Golden Globe Awards, private concerts, and the annual Sundance Film Festival, which, until 2026, was held at the foot of the Rocky Mountains in Park City, Utah. Sundance, started by the late actor Robert Redford, is a week-long party celebrating the best in film from that year, with a who’s who of Hollywood descending on this snowy, storybook town.

In 2012, worlds collided when (my now-husband) Brian and I first started dating, because he coincidentally hosted the hardest-to-get-into party at Sundance. Just a few months into dating, he invited me to come along. I said yes before even checking to see if I had enough vacation days. Somehow I convinced my editor that I should go and cover all the celebrity happenings. I also told her it would cost the company nothing because I had been invited by a film studio, which was a total lie—I put it all on my credit card and told myself I’d figure it out later. Luckily, she said yes!

Brian and I had already been on a few trips, but it was getting harder to pretend I was headed to the gym for a workout when I really was going to find a lobby loo. Eventually I had to come clean, because saying I was going to hit the gym at 11 p.m. after a couple of cocktails wasn’t passing his BS meter.

But the good news was, my flares were intermittent. And because I had a really good poker face, he had no clue just how bad things could get. Plus, over the eighteen months since my symptoms first wreaked havoc on my life, I’d become a pro on how to mentally map out bathrooms wherever we went. I’d also order sparingly at dinner. I didn’t want to be holed up in some bathroom while he was out there wondering what he’d gotten himself into. Trying to hide my disease while also managing my day-to-day emotional spirals, however, became a lot to handle.

We finally made it to Park City, and from our balcony we could see the snow-covered mountains in the distance and the beautifully dressed celebrities in their winter wear dotting the streets below us. I couldn’t wait to wear an outfit I had been saving in my suitcase—the all-white look. We’re talking a faux fur vest and matching beanie, topped off with the cutest low-rise skinny jeans. I looked like an influencer before there even were influencers, y’all!

As Brian and I made our way to a rooftop party, I suddenly felt the urge. My eyes immediately darted to a hallway on the other side of these massive sliding glass doors. It was a single-stall bathroom and the line was too long. I had to make a move, and fast. I tugged on Brian’s sweater and whispered, “I need to run back to the hotel.” He understood it was urgent code for needing to use the bathroom. He handed me our room key, and I couldn’t have left any faster.

As I started speed-walking toward our hotel, the soft buzz of the alcohol slowly began to dissipate, and my intermittent depression began to creep in. Here we were, in this beautiful city at a dreamlike event, and it had been interrupted once again by my disease. Can’t I catch a break? I thought.

As I crossed the street to our hotel, the need to go was only intensifying. Almost there, I said to myself. Squeeze your cheeks!

I got to the elevator bank and pushed the up button several times, as if that would make the elevator move faster. I painfully watched the numbers click at a glacial pace from floor to floor: eight . . . seven . . . six . . . And then, like a volcano about to erupt, my poor body just couldn’t keep it in. I prayed it would just be some flatulence.

Luckily, no one was around, but I was devastated, embarrassed, and defeated. When I got back to the hotel room I threw away those new, gorgeous jeans that now represented something so painful. I sat and cried.

“How much longer can I live like this?” I whimpered.

I didn’t feel like going back out. I had worked too hard to prevent ulcerative colitis from stealing my joy and taking over my life. That day, I had lost the battle. I had assumed that if I could map bathrooms in every space I entered, limit what I ate, and take my medicine, situations like this wouldn’t happen. So much of ulcerative colitis was accepting that most of this was totally out of my control. I texted Brian that I was going to skip the rest of the events that afternoon. He came back and just held me on the bed.

This was the only time I ever soiled myself. But I can’t tell you the countless times I’ve busted into coffee shops and restaurants to beg for the code to the bathroom only to be told I’d need to wait in line and buy something.

They say you’re supposed to know your nearest exit. But as someone living with bowel disease, I’ve always scouted the nearest bathroom. If I was going to be traveling, I’d time my meals so that I could avoid having to make a pit stop on long car rides, train trips, or flights. I’d even get dehydrated, because I’d be so nervous that even water would trigger me. I was withering away both physically and emotionally.

It sounds dramatic, and that’s because it is. But I have managed to find the humor. I can even laugh (a little) now at what happened at Sundance and say to myself, Well, at least Brad Pitt wasn’t standing in the elevator next to me.

#4946. Reformation’s Debut Home Collection Gives French Rugs an LA Attitude

For years, people shopping at Reformation have asked about something the brand didn’t sell. “One of the biggest comments that we get is that the stores look so beautiful and the rugs are amazing,” says Lauren Caris Cohan, the brand’s chief creative officer. Typically, she says, one question follows that observation: “Where can we get them?” Until now, the answer was nowhere; Thomas Coccimiglio, founder of the French rugmaker Les Editions de Tapis, is “really precious” about his designs for Reformation stores, Cohan says, and doesn’t reuse them for other clients.

Today, Reformation’s collaboration with Les Editions de Tapis makes the coveted rugs available to the public, with nine limited-edition styles, all made to order and priced between $1,000 and $5,000.

Long before he began outfitting Reformation showrooms, Coccimiglio spent more than 15 years as a textile designer in fashion, working in London, Paris, and New York. At Tory Burch, his trips to meet embroiderers in Italy and India eventually led him to rug-making, and during the pandemic, he left fashion for interiors, though his fashion background continues to influence him, even in the way he speaks about rugs: “I approach a rug almost as I would a piece of embroidery on a garment.”

Reformation opens more than 15 stores a year, Cohan says, and each one gets its own hand-sketched motif and custom colorway from Coccimiglio. “There’s a bit of a French sense of humor, a bone-dry sense of humor, in our [Reformation] brand DNA,” Cohan says, “that’s reflected in Thomas’ designs.”

For the new capsule collection of rugs, Reformation gave Coccimiglio full creative direction. The conversations that launched the collaboration involved Marie Antoinette, swans, a lot of pink and blue—all components also reflected in the partnering 12-piece clothing campaign, live today. To help inspire a cohesive thread linking Coccimiglio’s line to the ethos of the brand, he also revisited years of store designs for details to incorporate. “There’s something nostalgic about these pieces, almost like a little Proustian madeleine,” Coccimiglio says. “For customers who have experienced Reformation's stores over the years, these pieces might feel strangely familiar, almost iconic.”

The Chintz rugs set two crossed palm trees (Cohan’s idea, which Coccimiglio sketched by hand) between trailing vine borders, with herons and swans tucked at their roots. Swans Fans puts two black-outlined swans among spiky fan palms on a French blue foundation. Le Chat leaves a field of lilac almost entirely empty, save for one striped cat walking a broken black line. Le Classique has an ivory ground, two wavering oxblood borders, and a single dot, fitting anyone with French sensibilities sans menagerie.

The rugs are hand-knotted from wool, silk, and linen. Coccimiglio manufactures his larger pieces in India, where he finds the wool softer and better suited to big formats. Smaller editions come from Nepal, where Himalayan wool is denser, and the results are ornate, “like jewelry,” says Coccimiglio. While you could find a less expensive rug elsewhere, Cohan points out that Coccimiglio’s are designed for longevity of use—plus, the rugmaker’s own work outside the Reformation collaboration costs considerably more. “This is a much more accessible price point for something so detailed and so custom,” she says.

Cohan, who restores midcentury houses in Los Angeles with her husband Matt Jacoby under the name Bob Audrey, doesn’t picture a single kind of room to house these rugs. “You could have it in a midcentury house in the Hollywood Hills, but it also would look so stunning in [a] herringbone-floor apartment in New York City,” she says.

The campaign was shot in a house at the top of Wonderland Park in Laurel Canyon, a 1950s enclave of single-story midcentury houses that includes Pierre Koenig’s Case Study House No. 21. Coccimiglio, meanwhile, works from a 17th-century hôtel particulier in Dijon. It’s fitting that the premise of the collection enmeshes old-world France with modern LA, as Coccimiglio places his work “somewhere between these two worlds: French heritage and contemporary culture.”

The collection is available to shop on Reformation.com on Nov. 9.

#4945. Jensen Huang resigns as Nvidia CEO, exits board; shares plunge 13%

Kress has served as Nvidia’s CFO since September 2013 and previously held finance positions at Cisco and Microsoft. In Thursday’s announcement, newly appointed board chair Jones said the company expects its operations and product roadmap to continue without interruption.

Nvidia is scheduled to report its fiscal 2027 third-quarter financial results on Nov. 17.
